M36 - THE OWNER'S RATCHET-STOP FAMILY OVER THE FROZEN 192-CELL GRID. READ-ONLY.
2026-10-03 11:28:22 UTC. Prereg PREREG_M36_RATCHET_GRID.md (1d1dad3).

PRIMARY FILL MODEL, fixed by a venue fact: Polymarket has NO stop orders, so a sell placed
below the bid executes at once as a TAKER. Every stop exit is therefore a taker sell at the
bid that triggered it, paying 0.07p(1-p); re-buys are taker at the ask with the same fee.
The "atStop" column is the unachievable reference only - it is what carried M35's +0.1339
and it is NOT read. The one genuinely resting exit is the take-profit limit above the market
(zero fee, fills only once the bid reaches T + a tick).

---- PASS TEST on E2 pooled: 0 of 192 cells pass every condition
     -> FAIL: no cell clears the frozen bar.
---- PASS TEST on E1 taker: 0 of 192 cells pass every condition
     -> FAIL: no cell clears the frozen bar.
---- PASS TEST on E1 ask-touch: 0 of 192 cells pass every condition
     -> FAIL: no cell clears the frozen bar.
---- PASS TEST on RANDOM control (should FAIL; if it passes the exit rule alone is the "edge"): 0 of 192 cells pass every condition
     -> FAIL: no cell clears the frozen bar.

================================================================================
M36 VERDICT: FAIL ON EVERY ENTRY SET. 0 OF 192 CELLS POSITIVE ANYWHERE, AND THE RANDOM-ENTRY
CONTROL EXPLAINS WHY THE FAMILY LOOKED GOOD IN M35.

  entry set                     HOLD per $1    best cell per $1    best cell
  E2 pooled, 3,693 entries         -0.0063            -0.0048      a20 g15 R off T none   t -0.58
  E1 fixed15 taker, n 125          -0.0451            -0.0039      a10 g15 R off T none   t -0.08
  E1 fixed15 ask-touch, n 63       -0.2482            (all worse than the E1 taker figures)
  RANDOM-ENTRY CONTROL             -0.1091            -0.0683      a30 g10 R off T none   t -3.91
CORRECTION, 10-03 11:5x, V caught this and it was my error: the sentence that stood here said "not
one cell in any of the 192 is positive, on any entry set". THAT WAS WRONG. E2 arm FAV has 40 of 192
POSITIVE cells. I had inspected only the pooled, E1 and control blocks and generalised to "anywhere"
without opening the five per-arm blocks my own script had already written into this file.
The VERDICT does not change, but the reason does, and this is the correct one: on arm FAV every one
of those 40 positive cells is BELOW ITS OWN HOLD. FAV held returns +0.0281 per $1 (n 791, maxDD
-144.66, 7/10 green); the best ratchet cell on it is a30 g10 R-on T-none at +0.0258 with vsHOLD
-17.98 and t_paired -0.31. So the cells are positive only because FAV itself is positive, and none
of them beats holding - the PASS rule fails on the "beats HOLD paired" clause, not on positivity.
On the other four arms, on E2 pooled, on both E1 sets and on the control, 0 of 192 are positive.

THE CONTROL IS THE RESULT. On RANDOM entries - random side, random second, same candles - the exit
grid improves the book by +1,385 at $10 against its own HOLD, from -0.1091 to -0.0683 per $1. That
is a LARGER improvement than the exit grid gives the real fixed15 entries (+51.52 on E1, +54.47 on
E2 pooled). So the thing the owner's ratchet does is not find edge: it REDUCES EXPOSURE. Cutting a
position early makes a losing book lose less whether or not the entries carried any signal at all,
and the control shows it does so on entries that are pure noise by construction. Every "improvement
versus HOLD" in M35 and in this grid is that, and nothing more.

THE SHAPE OF THE GRID SAYS THE SAME THING THREE MORE TIMES:
 - R = OFF wins in every single top cell on every entry set. Re-buying always costs money, which is
   the M32 finding surviving the owner's correction and the whole parameter sweep.
 - T = none wins in every top cell. The take-profit - the one genuinely resting, zero-fee exit in
   the whole design - never helps.
 - the best arm levels are the HIGH ones (a20 on E2, a10-20 on E1, a30 on the control), i.e. the
   cells that arm latest and therefore trade least. The grid's own optimum is "do as little as
   possible", which is exposure reduction wearing a parameter.

AND THE atStop REFERENCE COLUMN SETTLES M35. It is hugely positive everywhere - +1,313.66 on E2
pooled's best cell - and, decisively, it is POSITIVE (+288.69) even on the RANDOM-ENTRY control.
A column that makes money on random entries is measuring the fill assumption, not the market. M35's
+0.1339 was exactly that column, and this is the proof it was never a strategy. Polymarket has no
stop orders, a sell below the bid is a taker sell at the bid, and priced that way the family loses.

WHAT I WOULD TELL THE OWNER: there is no version of the ratchet stop in this 192-cell family that
makes a profit on this data - not at any arm level, give-back, re-buy setting or take-profit. The
closest cells merely lose a little less than holding, and a random-entry control gets the same
benefit, which means that benefit is not edge. The honest next step is not another parameter: it is
London's real fills, where the question is whether a stop exit fills at the bid we can see.
Nothing live was touched and nothing here licenses a change.
